# Findings register (internal reviews)

Every finding from Avana's internal review and verification runs, one row each, with the run that found it, its status and how it was resolved. This register is the internal-review view: it holds every finding as soon as a run reports it, including findings that are open. A finding that is resolved or accepted here is mirrored as a `FINDING-NNN` entry in [5.3 Audit Findings](../spec/05-verification/03-audit-findings.md), which stays the official register. The upkeep rule is in [README.md](README.md#keeping-the-record-current).

Spec state at every run of 2026-11-09: decisions `Q-001`…`Q-240`. The custody re-verification `FV-CUS-R3` and the control re-verification `FV-CTL-R3` (2026-11-10) ran against the final spec, decisions `Q-001`…`Q-278`, as did the user-simulation re-run `SIM-R3`. Decisions `Q-241` onward were checked in [4.4 Design Decisions](../spec/04-security/04-design-decisions.md) and in the spec pages on 2026-11-09 (section 3). The AMM-neutral design review (run `AMMN-R1`, 2026-11-10, [combined report](design-review/amm-neutral/report.md)) ran against decisions `Q-001`…`Q-278` and the proposed AMM-neutral design; its findings are resolved by the final AMM-neutral design and its decisions, `Q-359`…`Q-368` among them, and wait for the re-verification runs against the rewritten pages. The parameter decisions of 2026-11-10 (`Q-391`…`Q-402`) removed the SettlementBudget, in-kind settlement, the idle-collateral mechanism, the on-chain health floors and the alert-only breakers; each finding whose fix used one of them carries a "Decision of 2026-11-10" note in its Resolution, and `AMMN-FLOW-16` is an accepted risk (`Q-396`).

In 5.3 the 63 findings found on 2026-11-09 are 60 entries, `FINDING-104`…`FINDING-163`: the 61 resolved findings are 57 entries with status Resolved in spec (`FINDING-112`, `FINDING-115` and `FINDING-120` each cover more than one finding), and the 2 Accepted risk findings plus the simulation's "Also observed" item are `FINDING-145`, `FINDING-160` and `FINDING-163`, Accepted risk. The three findings of `FV-CUS-R3` (`FV-CUS-F-07`…`F-09`) are `FINDING-178`…`FINDING-180`, the seven CVL alignment findings (`CVL-F-01`…`CVL-F-07`, found on 2026-11-10 by checking the CVL rules against the final 5.2 text) are `FINDING-181`…`FINDING-187`, and `SIM-GAP-14` is `FINDING-188`, all Resolved in spec. The ten findings of `FV-LIQ-R3` (`FV-LIQ-F-10`…`F-19`) are `FINDING-189`…`FINDING-198`, `MATH-ST-84` is `FINDING-199` and `HYP-85` is `FINDING-200`; `MATH-ST-58` and `MATH-ST-75` share `FINDING-195` and `FINDING-190` with `FV-LIQ-F-16` and `F-11`, all Resolved in spec. The findings of `FV-CTL-R3` (`FV-CTL-F-18`…`F-24`) and `SIM-GAP-13` of `SIM-R3`, resolved on 2026-11-10, are `FINDING-170`…`FINDING-177`. The 82 findings of `AMMN-R1` are `FINDING-201`…`FINDING-282`, in the order `AMMN-RT-01`…`16` (`FINDING-201`…`216`), `AMMN-FLOW-01`…`26` (`217`…`242`), `AMMN-GAS-01`…`16` (`243`…`258`), `AMMN-INT-01`…`08` (`259`…`266`), `AMMN-ARCH-01`…`10` (`267`…`276`) and `AMMN-SM-01`…`06` (`277`…`282`), all Resolved in spec — re-verification pending. The spec-stage counterexamples `SIM-02H`, `SIM-04C` and `QNT-F1`, which had no finding before, are `FINDING-283`…`FINDING-285`, Accepted risk (`Q-368`). `FV-CUS-F-10` is `FINDING-286` and `FV-CTL-F-25`…`F-29` are `FINDING-287`…`FINDING-291`, all Resolved in spec — re-verification pending. `HYP-36` is `FINDING-292`, Resolved in spec — re-verification pending.

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Open — scheduled for the spec-fix pass (Q-241 onward) | The finding stands against the spec as written. Where a decision is already recorded, the Resolution column names it and the rules it changes. |
| Resolved in spec — re-verification pending | Every rule the fix changes carries the decision in the spec pages, and the finding is mirrored in 5.3; the proving check has not yet passed in a run logged in `RUNS.md` against the edited pages. It moves to Resolved in spec when that run passes. |
| Resolved in spec | The spec rule has changed and the check in "Verified by" passes against the changed rule. |
| Accepted risk | A recorded decision keeps the rule and accepts the residual risk, with the bound stated. |
| Rejected | The finding was re-examined and does not hold; the Resolution says why. |

For concentrated liquidity the oracle square-root ratio is clamped to `[sqrtRatio(tickLower), sqrtRatio(tickUpper)]`, amounts are floored then reduced by 1 raw unit, tick alignment uses signed math, and uncollected fees are excluded. | `AMMN-RT-03` | [AMMN-R1](design-review/RUNS.md), [red team](design-review/amm-neutral/red-team.md) | 2026-11-10 | High | A standard Spoke turns code-level liquidation guarantees into Aave-side configuration | `INV-011`, `INV-014`, `INV-029`, `ATTACK-002`, `ATTACK-014`, `ATTACK-021`, `ATTACK-120`, `INV-093`, `REQ-RISK-078`, `REQ-ADAPTER-029`, `REQ-CONFIG-076`, `REQ-VTOKEN-006`, `REQ-VTOKEN-008`, `REQ-VTOKEN-009`, `REQ-VTOKEN-040`, `REQ-VTOKEN-041` | Resolved in spec — re-verification pending | Resolved by `Q-362`, `Q-281`, `Q-300`, `Q-363`: Every CDP Spoke is a standard, unmodified Aave V4 Spoke. The listing AIP sets `receiveSharesEnabled = false` on every Avana collateral reserve, `liquidationFee = 0` (or paid to Avana's fee receiver) and no reinvestment controller, and the RiskModule drift check compares each setting on every new-activity call. Drift trips a breaker that stops new activity, never liquidation. The vault token's transfer rule allows only the AaveAdapter's mint and burn, proxy to home Hub on supply, home Hub to proxy on withdrawal, and home Hub to LPVaultSwap on seizure (`REQ-VTOKEN-025`), so any other liquidator's `liquidationCall` reverts on the token transfer. The residual risk runs from an Aave DAO change that enables one of the settings until the drift check trips `CONFIG_DRIFT`, which runs on every Avana entry point and on a per-block keeper poke; a seizure outside LPVaultSwap in that window is accepted and bounded by the Spoke draw caps, stated in 4.3 (`EXT-001`). Changed rules: `INV-093`, `INV-011`, `INV-029`, `ATTACK-021`, `ATTACK-155`, `REQ-RISK-078`, `REQ-ADAPTER-029`, `REQ-CONFIG-076`, `REQ-VTOKEN-006`, `REQ-VTOKEN-008`, `REQ-VTOKEN-009`, `REQ-VTOKEN-040`, `REQ-VTOKEN-041`. Mirrored as `FINDING-203`. | Not yet run against the rewritten pages: `TEST-268`, `PROP-078` |

| `AMMN-RT-13` | [AMMN-R1](design-review/RUNS.md), [red team](design-review/amm-neutral/red-team.md) | 2026-11-10 | Medium | A shared SettlementBudget window let one Spoke's liquidations starve another's | `INV-037`, `INV-062`, `INV-075`, `INV-097`, `SPEC-SET-007`, `REQ-BUDGET-027`, `Q-387`, `SPEC-BRK-004`, `SPEC-SET-006`, `REQ-SWAP-029`, `REQ-SWAP-061`, `SPEC-SET-003`, `SPEC-SET-005`, `REQ-BUDGET-002` | Resolved in spec — re-verification pending | Resolved by `Q-362`, `Q-343`, `Q-363`: SettlementBudget windows are per market, with a running sum per key, so one market cannot use another's allowance. Changed rules: `INV-097`, `INV-037`, `ATTACK-165`, `SPEC-BRK-004`, `SPEC-SET-006`, `REQ-SWAP-029`, `REQ-SWAP-061`, `SPEC-SET-003`, `SPEC-SET-005`, `REQ-BUDGET-002`. Mirrored as `FINDING-213`. Fix pass (2026-11-10, [final re-check](design-review/amm-neutral/final-recheck.md#fix-pass)): the balance per asset is shared and windows bound each market's draws; each market has a reserved floor, a draw reverts `InsufficientBudget` when it would leave the balance below the other markets' reserves left, and `INV-097` states what holds: no market's draws exceed its window, the draws of an asset stay within the asset window and the balance, `reservedSum ≤ balance`, and no draw for one market lowers another market's reserve. Resolved, citing `SPEC-SET-007`, `REQ-BUDGET-027`, `INV-097`, `Q-387` (also `SPEC-SET-006`, `REQ-BUDGET-002`); new attack N3 is resolved by the same rules. Decision of 2026-11-10 (`Q-397`): the SettlementBudget is removed, so this fix no longer applies; the caller sizes its funding up front and pays any rounding gap through its shortfall top-up, and no Avana contract funds a settlement gap (`INV-037`). | Not yet run against the rewritten pages: `TEST-277`, `PROP-084` |

Tokenized stocks are not listed at launch (launch is a Stable LP Hub on Ethereum and on Base). Before any listing on a Volatile LP Hub: a market-hours-aware feed that blocks new risk while the reference market is closed, an issuer allowlist covering LPVaultSwap, the swap routes and the LPVault, and offboarding ahead of a corporate action. | `AMMN-FLOW-02` | [AMMN-R1](design-review/RUNS.md), [flow walkthroughs](design-review/amm-neutral/flow-walkthroughs.md) | 2026-11-10 | Critical | Liquidation and deleverage on Base assumed a GHO FlashMinter, GSM routes and a $1 GHO | `LIQ-015`, `LIQ-029`, `LIQ-070`, `MKT-015`, `INV-040`, `INV-070`, `INV-095`, `REQ-SWAP-011`, `REQ-SWAP-017`, `SPEC-LIQ-004`, `SPEC-LIQ-005`, `SPEC-LIQ-006`, `REQ-SWAP-012`, `REQ-FLASH-001`, `REQ-FLASH-007`, `REQ-FLASH-010`, `SPEC-LIQ-002` | Resolved in spec — re-verification pending | Resolved by `Q-362`, `Q-365`, `Q-333`, `Q-334`, `Q-363`: LPVaultSwap flash-borrows the debt asset itself through `IFlashSource`, implemented with the Aave V3 Pool `flashLoan` (single asset, mode 0) on every chain. Before the loan it checks available liquidity and the premium against `maxFlashFeeBps`; in the callback it checks initiator, caller and the active context. The flash source is never the Hub of the CDP Spoke being liquidated, the implementation is replaceable through the timelock, and when flash liquidity is short the caller funds the debt. The GhoFlashMinter, the GSM as a primary route and the leg that buys the debt with GHO are removed. LPVaultSwap swaps the unwound tokens over the swap routes into the debt asset with a per-leg oracle `minOut`, repays the flash loan plus premium, and pays the leftover in the debt asset repaid, in this order: Avana's fee (20 % of the oracle gain, capped by the realized surplus), the keeper reward, then the borrower surplus (`Q-337`, 2.11 `SPEC-LIQ-009`, `SPEC-LIQ-022`). GHO is used only when the debt is GHO. Changed rules: `INV-040`, `INV-095`, `INV-070`, `ATTACK-170`, `REQ-SWAP-011`, `REQ-SWAP-017`, `SPEC-LIQ-004`, `SPEC-LIQ-005`, `SPEC-LIQ-006`, `REQ-SWAP-012`, `REQ-FLASH-001`, `REQ-FLASH-007`, `REQ-FLASH-010`, `SPEC-LIQ-002`. Mirrored as `FINDING-218`. | Not yet run against the rewritten pages: `TEST-267`, `TEST-280`, `PROP-080` |
| `AMMN-FLOW-03` | [AMMN-R1](design-review/RUNS.md), [flow walkthroughs](design-review/amm-neutral/flow-walkthroughs.md) | 2026-11-10 | High | No L2 sequencer gate, and Base feed heartbeats failed the listing rule | `MKT-005`, `REQ-ORACLE-006`, `REQ-ORACLE-054`, `LIQ-007`, `INV-098`, `INV-023`, `REQ-CHAIN-003`, `REQ-ORACLE-099`, `SPEC-ORC-019`, `LIQ-086`, `REQ-RISK-081` | Resolved in spec — re-verification pending | Resolved by `Q-362`, `Q-364`, `Q-287`, `Q-363`, `Q-365`: Each L2 chain has a sequencer-uptime feed (Base `0xBCF85224fc0756B9Fa45aA7892530B47e10b6433`) and a grace period after restart. While the sequencer is down or inside the grace period no new risk opens; liquidation keeps its stress rules. A feed is fresh while its age is at most its own heartbeat plus a margin (Chainlink stablecoin feeds: 82,800–86,400 s); the fixed 3,600 s rule is removed. Borrow and withdraw also require an independent second source (a deep-pool TWAP or a second oracle network) to agree within the deviation band. Liquidation keeps its stress rules: stale collateral counts as 0 from Expired and stale debt uses its stress price. Changed rules: `INV-098`, `INV-023`, `ATTACK-171`, `REQ-CHAIN-003`, `REQ-ORACLE-099`, `SPEC-ORC-019`, `LIQ-086`, `REQ-RISK-081`. Before the loan it checks available liquidity and the premium against `maxFlashFeeBps`; in the callback it checks initiator, caller and the active context. The flash source is never the Hub of the CDP Spoke being liquidated, the implementation is replaceable through the timelock, and when flash liquidity is short the caller funds the debt. The GhoFlashMinter, the GSM as a primary route and the leg that buys the debt with GHO are removed. LPVaultSwap swaps the unwound tokens over the swap routes into the debt asset with a per-leg oracle `minOut`, repays the flash loan plus premium, and pays the leftover in the debt asset repaid, in this order: Avana's fee (20 % of the oracle gain, capped by the realized surplus), the keeper reward, then the borrower surplus (`Q-337`, 2.11 `SPEC-LIQ-009`, `SPEC-LIQ-022`). GHO is used only when the debt is GHO. Each token class has route requirements, and each market's debt cap follows the measured depth of its own routes. Changed rules: `INV-083`, `INV-070`, `ATTACK-182`, `REQ-SWAP-011`, `REQ-SWAP-017`, `SPEC-LIQ-004`, `SPEC-LIQ-005`, `SPEC-LIQ-006`, `REQ-SWAP-022`, `REQ-ROUTE-001`, `REQ-ROUTE-002`, `REQ-ROUTE-036`, `REQ-ROUTE-039`. Mirrored as `FINDING-230`. | Not yet run against the rewritten pages: `TEST-280`, `TEST-281` |

Changed rules: `INV-071`, `INV-094`, `ATTACK-183`, `REQ-HUB-021`, `REQ-HUB-103`, `REQ-FLR-001`, `REQ-RISK-082`, `SPEC-CAP-010`, `DEF-015`, `DEF-020`. | `AMMN-FLOW-16` | [AMMN-R1](design-review/RUNS.md), [flow walkthroughs](design-review/amm-neutral/flow-walkthroughs.md) | 2026-11-10 | Medium | A debt-free LP could fill every cap at no cost | `SPEC-CAP-005`, `SPEC-BACK-004`, `Q-157`, `INV-092`, `REQ-ADAPTER-095`, `REQ-RISK-080` | Accepted risk | Accepted risk by decision `Q-396` (2026-11-10). A debt-free LP can hold room under `marketUsdCap`, `tokenUsdCap` and `depositorUsdCap` without borrowing, and the protocol has no on-chain mechanism that releases that room: the idle mechanism (`releaseIdle`, the `Idle` cap bucket, `idleUsdCap`, `idleBookingSeconds`, `IDLE_UTILIZATION_BPS`) is removed. Bound: the squatter's capital is locked at full value, earns only pool fees, and blocks only new pledges in its market and Hub, never exits, repay or liquidation; the top-up slice stays reserved for indebted accounts, so rescues still pass. Handling: caps are set at listing for expected demand, monitoring alerts on a cap held near full by debt-free accounts, and governance raises caps through the timelock. Earlier fixes: `Q-361`, `Q-363` keyed cap counters by Hub in the one ledger. Changed rules: `INV-092`, `ATTACK-184`, `REQ-ADAPTER-095`, `REQ-RISK-080`. Mirrored as `FINDING-232`. | Not applicable (accepted risk) |

Avana has no minimum debt on any chain; Avana's own approved keepers liquidate every account and Avana pays the gas, with the permissionless fallback kept, so liquidation never depends on a keeper's profit. The 16-record cap stays because one liquidation of the largest account must fit the per-transaction gas limit: `G = 1.31M + 0.24M × 16 = 5.15M ≤ 16,777,216`. `SPEC-CHAIN-001` states only the per-chain gas model in gas units. Changed rules: `INV-096`, `SPEC-CHAIN-001`, `REQ-RISK-083`, `LIQ-087`, `REQ-RISK-077`, `SPEC-CAP-009`, and in 2.11 `REQ-SWAP-032` (Avana's keepers on every Hub) and `REQ-SWAP-101` (no minimum debt; the record cap sized to one transaction); checked by MATH-ST-108. Before the loan it checks available liquidity and the premium against `maxFlashFeeBps`; in the callback it checks initiator, caller and the active context. The flash source is never the Hub of the CDP Spoke being liquidated, the implementation is replaceable through the timelock, and when flash liquidity is short the caller funds the debt. Drift trips a breaker that stops new activity, never liquidation. The vault token's transfer rule allows only the AaveAdapter's mint and burn, proxy to home Hub on supply, home Hub to proxy on withdrawal, and home Hub to LPVaultSwap on seizure (`REQ-VTOKEN-025`), so any other liquidator's `liquidationCall` reverts on the token transfer. Fix pass (2026-11-10, [final re-check](design-review/amm-neutral/final-recheck.md#fix-pass)): the window is the Aave change until `CONFIG_DRIFT` trips; seizures outside LPVaultSwap in it are an accepted residual bounded by the Spoke draw caps (`INV-093`, `ATTACK-155`). | Not yet run against the rewritten pages: `TEST-268`, `PROP-078` |

Token exposure is counted per funding Hub over every Spoke that draws from it. Changed rules: `INV-075`, `INV-092`, `REQ-COLL-026`, `REQ-RISK-079`, `SPEC-CAP-002`, `SPEC-CAP-007`. | `SIM-02H` | [Spec-stage simulation](../spec/05-verification/simulation/README.md) | 2026-11-09 | Low | A 24-hour failure of both USDC routes with no standby switch liquidated accounts healthy at market prices | `Q-247`, `Q-248`, `Q-368`, `REQ-ARCH-188`, `REQ-ARCH-202`, `REQ-ORACLE-093` | Accepted risk | Accepted by `Q-368`: Stress valuation stays: liquidation must not trust a market with no fresh route. The loss is bounded by the liquidation bonus on fresh collateral, and the pause guardian's standby switch (`useStandbyRoute`, effective at once) ends the outage; a switch within 30 minutes removes every forced liquidation (`SIM-02I`). The bound and the 30-minute switch target are the Guardian's playbook duty. Rules: `REQ-ARCH-188`, `REQ-ARCH-202`, `REQ-ORACLE-093`. Mirrored as `FINDING-283`. | `TEST-230`, `TEST-231`; spec-stage: `SIM-02H` |
| `SIM-04C` | [Spec-stage simulation](../spec/05-verification/simulation/README.md) | 2026-11-09 | Medium | With no approved keeper for 48 hours, the permissionless fallback left tail bad debt | `Q-082`, `Q-213`, `Q-368`, `REQ-SWAP-034`, `LIQ-087`, `REQ-RISK-083`, `SPEC-CHAIN-001` | Accepted risk | Accepted by `Q-368`: The permissionless fallback is a liveness backstop that runs only where it pays; the duty to clear unprofitable accounts sits with the approved keepers (floor of 3 per chain, `INV-048`) and the standby keeper's agreement. Since `Q-381` Avana has no minimum debt, and Avana's own keepers liquidate every account and pay the gas, so clearing an account never depends on its profitability (`INV-096`). Residual bad debt goes to the Coverage Spoke of the market's (Hub, debt asset). Rules: `REQ-SWAP-034`, `LIQ-087`, `REQ-RISK-083`, `SPEC-CHAIN-001`. Mirrored as `FINDING-284`. | `TEST-098`, `TEST-215`, `TEST-278`; spec-stage: `SIM-04C` |

A hostile LPVaultSwap is inside the upgrade blast radius of `INV-018`: it needs a timelocked upgrade at the normal delay, cancellable by every canceller, which is the bound accepted for every LPVaultSwap fault. Rules: `REQ-ORACLE-085`, `LIQ-053`, `REQ-ARCH-155`. Mirrored as `FINDING-285`. | `TEST-230`, `TEST-154`; spec-stage: `QNT-F1` |

### 2.1 Failures of the slow verification runs on code

Failures of the slow CI tier on `main` (deep invariant campaigns, Echidna, Medusa, hevm, Mythril), one row per root cause. None is a contract defect, so they are not counted in section 1 and are not mirrored in 5.3. Status "Resolved in verification code" means the cause was a property, harness, fixture, triage entry or CI setting; `contracts/src` is unchanged, and the "Verified by" checks pass on the fixed code. Runs: `contracts` 43653564553 and `security-slow` 43653574421, dispatched on `main` at `48864ef` on 2026-11-14.

| ID | Source run | Date found | Failed check | Root cause | Status | Fix | Verified by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SLOW-01` | `contracts` 43653564553: tests (ci profile) shard 9/30 and the deep campaign of `test/invariant/RiskModule.invariant.t.sol` ([foundry-tests](foundry-tests/README.md)) | 2026-11-15 | `invariant_TEST_095_noGateBypassed`: "a pre-check passed with a gate closed", shrunk to `setLegs([...], [0, 0])` then `withdraw(2)` (shard 9) or `withdraw(3235)` (deep) | Harness. `targetContract(handler)` exposed the setup helper `RiskModuleHandler.setLegs`, and the fuzzer set both leg feeds to address 0. Both seeds open every gate and then close the stale-feed gate by warping past the feed age. The RiskModule skips a leg whose feed is address 0 (an unused second leg, 2.7 step (5)), so with no named feed it had no age to check, while the handler's independent reading still counted the gate closed. A real LPOracle always reports the market's own feeds, and the oracle's own borrow gate also covers the served age. | Resolved in verification code | The handler takes the tokens and leg feeds in its constructor (`World.legTokens`, `World.legFeeds`) and has no setter; the invariant is unchanged | `test/unit/RiskModule.HandlerLegs.unit.t.sol` (both seeds refused, an all-open pre-check passes); the RiskModule invariant suite in CI |
| `SLOW-02` | `contracts` 43653564553: deep campaign of `test/invariant/Integration.invariant.t.sol` ([foundry-tests](foundry-tests/README.md)) | 2026-11-15 | `setUp()`: `vm.getCode: multiple matching artifacts found` | Fixture and CI. With several matching artifacts, `deployCode("X.sol:X")` keeps only the copy in the running test's compiler profile. The `IntegrationHandler` compilation restriction (legacy pipeline, 200 runs) puts the test in a non-default profile. The deep job compiled only the file's import graph (the invariant profile's `match_path`), where a contract the fixtures deploy by name and never import (PayoutEscrow, LPVaultSwap, Lens, DexPathRoute) is compiled only in the profiles of the suites that import it: two copies elsewhere and none in the test's profile. The ci shards reuse the full build, which has that copy, so they passed. | Resolved in verification code | Fixtures name the default profile for the unrestricted contracts they deploy by name (`X.sol:X:default`), the production build, in every build; the deep invariant jobs reuse the build job's full output (`forge-out`), as the shards do | The deep Integration campaign in the slow-tier dispatch on the fix branch; every full-stack suite in CI |
| `SLOW-03` | `security-slow` 43653574421: Echidna and Medusa `DebtOracleProperties` ([echidna](echidna/README.md), [medusa](medusa/README.md)) | 2026-11-15 | `property_nonLiveServesAtLeastAccepted` (Medusa: `move(0, 64)`, one call) | Property. While Drifting it expected `max(p, b, A)`; `SPEC-ORC-018` and `DebtOracleEvaluation._serve` serve `max(min(p, b), A)`, the lower route never below the accepted price (one faulty route must not raise the debt price alone). Primary 149,985,000, alternate and accepted 99,990,000: the oracle served 99,990,000, as specified. | Resolved in verification code | The Drifting branch expects `max(min(p, b), A)` with `liquidationPaused`; Drifting is set only when both routes are valid and disagree, so both values are route values | `test/unit/DebtOracle.FuzzHarness.unit.t.sol`; Echidna and Medusa in the slow-tier dispatch on the fix branch |
| `SLOW-04` | `security-slow` 43653574421 (and 43595414156): Medusa `LPOracleProperties` ([medusa](medusa/README.md)) | 2026-11-14 | `property_servedByState`: Drifting served 199,999,800 above the accepted 198,999,801 | Property. It required a Drifting market to serve at most the accepted price. `SPEC-ORC-017` serves the fresh price in Live and Drifting, or `min(lower, fresh)` while a token disagrees; the accepted price enters only the Cached value, and the RiskModule caps borrowing at `min(served, A)` (`REQ-RISK-059`). | Resolved in verification code | The property requires exactly the `SPEC-ORC-017` value in Live and Drifting (the target's `served()` also returns `fresh`, `lower` and `pauseActive` or `disagreementExpired` from the same evaluation); Expired 1 and Cached `A` are unchanged | `test/unit/LPOracle.FuzzHarness.unit.t.sol` (both runs' sequences); Medusa in the slow-tier dispatch on the fix branch |
| `SLOW-05` | `security-slow` 43653574421: Mythril group 10 ([mythril](mythril/README.md)) | 2026-11-15 | SWC-116 (Low) in `LensPriceModule.getDebtPriceState(address,uint256)`, fingerprint `155f63bab850403b`, reported twice | Tool false positive. `block.timestamp` is the report's block stamp, the `updatedAt` of a fixed-price reserve and the clock of the age and the `borrowFresh` and `liquidationFresh` flags (execution's own freshness rules through `PriceAge`, added with the Lens price-age change). The function is a view of the read-only Lens module, reached by STATICCALL; it moves nothing and decides nothing on chain. | Resolved in verification code | Accepted entry in `contracts/security/mythril-triage.json` (approved, Josue, 2026-11-14) | The run's group 10 report gated with the triage file: pass, 0 blocking, 12 accepted |
| `SLOW-06` | `security-slow` 43653574421: hevm `harness:PositionProxyProperties` ([hevm](hevm/README.md)) | 2026-11-15 | `adapterCall(uint128)` killed at the 14 GB memory cap; `revertPassthrough(uint256,bool)` explored only partially | Harness and tool limits. `ProxyCallTarget` stores the whole calldata in a `bytes` slot, so the symbolic argument became a symbolic storage write carried back through the proxy's return path, which the harness never checks. `revertPassthrough` built calldata of 4 or 36 bytes with one ternary, which hevm merged into one symbolically sized copy. | Resolved in verification code | The harness calls `ProxyPingTarget`, which records the caller, value and call count only, and the argument of `adapterCall` stays symbolic; `revertPassthrough` makes one call per case | hevm in the slow-tier dispatch on the fix branch; Echidna and Medusa run the same harness |
| `SLOW-07` | `security-slow` 43653574421: hevm `harness:FlashLiquidatorProperties` ([hevm](hevm/README.md)) | 2026-11-15 | `noMinterCallback(address,uint256,uint256)` explored only partially (34 symbolic-size copies, the iteration bound, symbolic SLOADs) | Tool limit. The symbolic `initiator`, the first argument, shares the first calldata word with the selector, and hevm 0.58 could not resolve the selector, so it explored the helper's other functions. | Resolved in verification code | The initiator is one of the four addresses the helper knows (this caller, the helper, the Pool, LPVaultSwap), with amount and fee symbolic. The helper has no `onFlashLoan`, so its dispatcher refuses the call whatever the arguments, and arbitrary initiators stay covered by `testFuzz_TEST_292_noMinterCallback` and the runtime-code check of TEST-292 | hevm in the slow-tier dispatch on the fix branch; `test/fuzz/FlashLiquidator.fuzz.t.sol` |

### 2.2 Mutation-testing survivors

Survivors of the mutation gate on code (`mutation-gate.yml`): `slither-mutate` mutants of `contracts/src` that no test killed, and Necessist removals from `contracts/test` after which the test still passed. A survivor is not a contract defect, so these rows are not counted in section 1 and are not mirrored in 5.3. Source runs: `mutation-gate` 43492416937 on `main` at `aa340fb` (2026-11-13; layer 2.1 `slither-mutate` and 176 Necessist jobs) and 43653570827 at `48864ef` (2026-11-14). Triage: first pass at `fda9bed`, second pass at `6805212` (2026-11-15). The rows below are the groups of [contracts/security/slither-mutate-triage.json](../../contracts/security/slither-mutate-triage.json) (`SM-…`) and [contracts/security/necessist-triage.json](../../contracts/security/necessist-triage.json) (`NE-…`), which list every mutant or removal with its line, text and full reason. Equivalent and noise classes wait for approval (`approval: pending` in the JSON). The `slither-mutate` survivors of `LPOracleCL*`, `LPOracleCalls` and the other contracts whose test set was fixed after the run are triaged after the next run.

| equivalent-or-harmless | 1,011 |
| noise | 50 |
| real-test-gap | 386 |
| superseded | 4 |
| test set fixed; needs the next mutation-gate run | 227 |
| not triaged | 0 |
| **All survivors** | **1,678** |

| Necessist class | Removals |

Status: "Resolved in verification code" means a test that fails against the mutant was added and passes on the code (targeted `forge test`, 2026-11-15); the next `mutation-gate` run confirms the kill. "Accepted, approval pending" is an equivalent or noise survivor whose reason is recorded. "Open" rows need a test strengthened or a re-run.

| `MUT-01` | Second-pass triage of `slither-mutate` survivors of `DebtOracle` (`SM` entries of `_recordLeg`) | 2026-11-15 | Low | A poke whose feed-definition read fails after the route feeds were read overwrites every leg record with round 0 and time 0 (REQ-ORACLE-031 says unread legs keep their record) | Fixed in code | Fixed in [pull request 74](https://github.com/Avana-Labs/avana-monorepo/pull/74): `_recordSeen` writes nothing when the configuration read failed (`R_CONFIG_UNAVAILABLE`). Effect before the fix: on the next poke a leg returning on the first round of a new phase loses its recovery evidence, and a leg whose round did not change is looked up as new (SPEC-ORC-014) | `test/unit/DebtOracle.SeenRecords.unit.t.sol` (both tests fail without the fix) |

## 3. Decisions recorded after the runs

Checked on 2026-11-09 at 22:10 EST, after the re-runs. 4.4 records decisions `Q-241`…`Q-278` from the spec-fix pass, and the pass was still writing them into the rule pages: 2.3, 2.12, 2.14, 2.17, 3.8, 3.9 and 4.2 already cite some of them, and the remaining pages cite none. No run logged in a `RUNS.md` has yet checked the changed rules; the `-FIX` checks that pass in `FV-CUS-R2`, `FV-CTL-R2` and `FV-LIQ-R2` model the recommended rules, not the edited pages. At that check no open finding was Resolved yet; the status update below records the state after the rule pages were written. Each finding moves to Resolved in spec when its rule pages carry the decision and its fix check passes in a logged run against those pages.

`Q-277` keeps `minPledgeUsd` at $10 with no rule change, so `DR-14` and `SIM-GAP-10` are Accepted risk now.

Status update after the rule pages of the spec-fix pass were written and integrated (2026-11-09): every rule each fix changes now carries its decision in the spec pages, and the pages were checked against each other, against the interfaces under `contracts/src` and against the decisions, and every changed rule was re-read and accepted in `trace.py`. No run logged in a `RUNS.md` has yet run the proving checks against the edited pages, so under rule 3 of the upkeep rule ([README.md](README.md#keeping-the-record-current)) each such finding is **Resolved in spec — re-verification pending**; it becomes Resolved in spec when the next verification run against the edited pages passes and is logged. Its Resolution names the decision and the changed rule IDs, and "Verified by" keeps the `-FIX` model checks that passed in the logged runs (which modelled the recommended rules, not the edited text) and adds the implementation-stage `TEST-…` and `PROP-…` checks of 5.1 and 5.2 that prove the fix on code. Every resolved (re-verification pending) and Accepted risk finding is mirrored in 5.3 as `FINDING-104`…`FINDING-163`: findings that share one fix share one entry (`FV-CTL-F-03`, `FV-LIQ-F-01` and `DR-06` as `FINDING-112`; `FV-CTL-F-07` and `DR-18` as `FINDING-115`; `FV-CTL-F-12` and `FV-LIQ-F-07` as `FINDING-120`), and the simulation's "Also observed" item is `FINDING-163` (Accepted risk, `Q-276`). Options a decision declined (for example on-chain enforcement of the tail cap, a keeper-only `settlePending`, a 3-day delay floor) are recorded with their reasons in 4.4 and get no `FINDING` entry.

Findings with no decision of their own: `FV-CUS-F-03`, `FV-CUS-F-04`, `FV-CUS-F-05`, `FV-CUS-F-06`, `FV-CTL-F-13`, `FV-CTL-F-16`, `DR-20`; they are text corrections under earlier decisions. `FV-CUS-F-06` and `FV-CTL-F-16` change the methodology in `formal-verification/methodology.md`, not a spec page.

The simulation's "Also observed" item (one account per wallet spreads one market's state to every market) is not one of its twelve gaps; `Q-276` keeps one account per wallet per Borrow Spoke.

## 4. Findings reported by more than one run

Each run reports independently, so some findings describe the same rule. They keep separate rows and share a resolution.

| Rule | Findings |
|---|---|
| `normalDelay` lower bound and the short-delay list | `FV-CTL-F-06`, `FV-CTL-F-07`, `DR-18` |
| Two-leg slippage at the minimum bonus | `FV-LIQ-F-01`, `DR-06`; `FV-CTL-F-03` for the buy-slippage tighten bound |
| Circuit breakers and address freeze | `FV-CTL-F-04`, `FV-CTL-F-17`, `DR-04` |
| Swap minimums and Avana's fee | `FV-LIQ-F-06`, `DR-07` |
| `REQ-CONFIG-050` and zero caps | `FV-CTL-F-12`, `FV-LIQ-F-07` |
| `minPledgeUsd` of $10 | `DR-14`, `SIM-GAP-10` |
| Rescue top-ups refused | `DR-09`, `SIM-GAP-03`, `SIM-GAP-09` |
| Debt-price failover and long halts | `DR-03`, `SIM-GAP-06` |
| `borrowPriceMaxAge` against feed heartbeats | `SIM-GAP-01`, `SIM-GAP-05`, `DR-20` (last item) |
| Feed heartbeats and the sequencer gate | `AMMN-INT-01`, `AMMN-FLOW-03`, `AMMN-INT-06`, `AMMN-ARCH-05` |
| Exact market shape and the range clamp | `AMMN-RT-01`, `AMMN-RT-02`, `AMMN-FLOW-08` |
| PositionProxy model (no beacon, no migration) | `AMMN-RT-04`, `AMMN-RT-16`, `AMMN-FLOW-04`, `AMMN-SM-03`, `AMMN-ARCH-09` |
| Flash source and settlement asset on Base | `AMMN-FLOW-02`, `AMMN-INT-05`, `AMMN-GAS-06`, `AMMN-ARCH-04`, `AMMN-INT-03` |
| Standard Spoke and its three settings | `AMMN-RT-03`, `AMMN-SM-04`, `AMMN-INT-04`, `AMMN-INT-07`, `AMMN-ARCH-07`, `AMMN-ARCH-10`, `AMMN-FLOW-13` |
| Lock membership and ledger scope | `AMMN-FLOW-01`, `AMMN-GAS-09`, `AMMN-ARCH-01`, `AMMN-RT-05` |
| Cross-Hub credit lines and draw caps | `AMMN-SM-01`, `AMMN-SM-02`, `AMMN-RT-11`, `AMMN-FLOW-05`, `AMMN-FLOW-06` |
| Uniswap v4 removal hooks | `AMMN-RT-06`, `AMMN-GAS-03` |
| ProxyManager with several Adapters | `AMMN-RT-10`, `AMMN-FLOW-18` |
| Spoke topology | `AMMN-FLOW-07`, `AMMN-ARCH-02` |
| Per-market SettlementBudget windows (budget removed, `Q-397`) | `AMMN-RT-13`, `AMMN-GAS-10` |
| Pause and token-cap scope across Hubs | `AMMN-SM-05`, `AMMN-SM-06`, `AMMN-FLOW-19` |
